# Annex XIII (c) – GENDER ASSESSMENT AND ACTION PLAN

## GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

### Gender norms block women from opportunities and resources to perform their roles effectively, especially since the livelihoods of women are completely dependent on agriculture. Lack of water in the dry season makes it difficult for women to collect water for household use, which is also difficult for women to find stable income from agricultural activities. If men move to other provinces, there is no support or sharing by men in regard to agricultural production and housework work. In addition, climate change has a significant impact on climate change adaptation, general and drought-adaptative solutions in agriculture in particular also make women more vulnerable than men. Women need access to credit, better access to timely information, learning opportunities and participation in community events.

#### 6. Ongoing initiatives addressing gender and ethnic minority issues in the target areas

154. Ninh Thuan Province has a total of 19 ODA projects, with a total of VND 38,420.48 billion (equivalent to $241,430,837 USD). Among the 19 projects, there are 15 on-going projects and 04 new projects. The provincial Women’s Union has been supported by many international organizations such as UNDP, USAID, ILO, etc., and Ninh Thuan Province has also received financial support from many INGOs with key donors including ACDI/VOCA, CARE, CIDA, DFAT, GIZ, JICA, KfW, Oxfam, Save the Children, SDC, UNFPA. Among 15 on-going projects and 21 new projects supported by INGOs in 2017, with a total budget of VND 346,493 USD*. Among the new projects, SNV supports the provincial Ninh Thuan Women’s Union to implement activities for women’s empowerment. The project aims at improving the capacity and representative women’s groups. Climate change is a part of the topics for awareness raising activities, but climate vulnerability is not specifically addressed in this project. However, the project addresses gender issues, but ethnic minorities in the project areas are included as one of the beneficiary groups.

155. Dak Nong province has issued permission to 17 INGOs to work, but up to December 2017 only 9 INGOs have been working in the province: CARE International, Concern Worldwide, World Vision, Fred Hollows Foundation, Mission Chance, EDE Consulting and PSI. Among these INGOs, only CARE International has a livelihood project for poor people including ethnic minorities*. Information about numbers of ODA projects is not available.

156. Dak lak province, from 2013 – 2017, the People’s Committee approved 48 projects for INGOs, for a total of VND 7,000 USD. These provide support for different areas including livelihood improvement and capacity building for women. The majority of these projects considered ethnic minority people as one of the target groups for project interventions.

## Information on development projects targeting gender equality and ethnic minorities is not available.

### Binh Thuan province: There are a total of 14 project supported by seven IFAD in some districts of the province, with a total budget about 14.059.646 USD. Information about project objectives on gender issues and ethnic minorities issue is not available.

#### Through consultation meetings with relevant departments of the provincial and district administration, it was determined that the following areas should be considered when implementing projects in ethnic minority areas, e.g North Thuan province: The Provincial Committee for Ethnic Affairs, the Department of Education and Training, and the Department of Agriculture and Rural Development. Given the lack of education among the Bahnar ethnic group in Ninh Thuan province, because they have limited education, therefore, when communicating with these communities, the project needs to use simple messages and a visual methodology to deliver messages.

#### The Women’s and Farmers Unions are two organizations who have significant experience in mainstreaming gender equality into their activities. Their experiences will be useful for this project in term of strengthening those groups under this project.

##### 161. Consultations with representatives from FAO and IFAD showed high potential for collaboration between this project and these two organizations. IFAD has worked with the provincial government to support the development of a women’s union in Ninh Thuan province, given their workload at district/commune level. IFAD also has experience in support common interest groups and credit unions, which are important mechanisms for the empowerment of collaborative groups of farmers and women. IFAD also learned that access to credit is a key constraint for many smallholder farmers and women’s groups in Ninh Thuan province. A critical issue is how to link farmers to existing credit services via collective mechanisms. Ninh Thuan has available microfinance institutions, but they are not well known by the target group or support vulnerable groups to access bank credit. IFAD also has experience in engaging private sector actors in the provision of financial services.

##### 162. In regard to FAO, the organization has a network of FFS in Viet Nam since 1994 through its IPM programs. In each FFS, a facilitator is critical, often via the Soil Plant Protection Division of DAFF. The project could benefit from this experience and the fact that the program has about $100 for 30 people. FAO also has cash transfer and voucher system interventions to households in response to droughts. Experience with this project could be useful for this project.

### Analysis

#### Stakeholder consultations, desk-top reviews and lessons amassed within UNDP from its past support in this area offer critical insights for designing projects that are responsive to the unique needs of women and ethnic minorities. These insights inform specific recommendations that are presented in the following section.

# Annex XIII (c) – GENDER ASSESSMENT AND ACTION PLAN GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL

## Needs unique to women and ethnic minorities

164. As presented in this document, FP and FS, and as widely recognized among development practitioners, vulnerable groups such as women and ethnic minorities require specialized support in building their capacity for adaptation to climate change. This is a fact of life reflecting the higher sensitivity to climate risks due to their greater dependence on climate-sensitive resources for their livelihoods; their lower adaptive capacity due to poverty, lack of capital (financial and human); and limited access to information and decision-making power. The gender analysis design of this project, as well as UNDP’s experiences in the past[2], support this commonly held perspective.

165. An important gender-differentiated factor that contributes to women's vulnerability, but also a factor that is important in designing an effective adaptation intervention, is “time poverty” that prevent many women from attending community meetings or training events. This time poverty may even extend beyond the household level and affect women’s ability to participate if they have no time to listen to radio or watch TV, which are the most common sources of advices for men (UN Women, 2016). Social/cultural norms also work against many women, and particularly ethnic women, in their capacity to participate in community meetings. This is a factor that is often suffered in this report. This is commonly the case despite the fact that women do share their views if circumstances force them to participate in these activities.

166. Consultations with women from ethnic minority groups during the design of this project revealed that many women were not adequately informed about the content and purpose of community meetings. This was particularly prominent among Ragiel and Cham people. This is likely to be a result of the fact that government agencies did not adequately encourage their participation or value their opinions or views.

167. Lastly, for effective participation of and delivery of development impact to ethnic minorities, it is important to recognize the language barrier that still exists. While the Government of Viet Nam has made significant progress in this area by developing a number of translation services (for example, employing extension workers from ethnic groups and translating public information into different languages), more is clearly needed in this area. Furthermore, as presented earlier, statistics show that compared to non-ethnic minority women, ethnic minority women are much more disadvantaged than non-poor Kinh people (the majority). This means that information and training services for ethnic minorities need not only be translated into different languages, but also be developed for non-literate groups. As will be described below, to this end, effective engagement of community champions and local support groups is vital.

## Institutional limitations

168. Earlier sections of this report indicate that most government institutions relevant for this project have gender mainstreaming policies and strategies in place, yet many of them do have considerable capacity constraints in identifying and implementing gender-responsive actions in their work[3]. For example, see UNDP’s Technical Brief “Opportunities to empower women with enhanced access to climate information services for transformative adaptation actions in Viet Nam’s agricultural sectors”

The context of their institutional mandates. In the context of this project, the following agencies are particularly important in ensuring gender and ethnic minorities' empowerment:

- **MARD** (province and district)
  - Centres for Agricultural Extension
    - Districts
- **DOLISA** (province and district)
- **Queensland Women's Commission for Ethnic Minorities**
- **Farmers' Union** (central, province, district and commune)
- **Viet Nam Women's Union (VWU)** (central, province, district and commune)

Among these agencies, VWU is considered the most significant supporter of women’s empowerment, although they too still require capacity building support, especially at the district and commune levels. The project will integrate a mentoring support mechanism with assistance from VWU at all levels.

## Recommendations

### 8. Recommendations

169. The general objectives of this gender assessment and action plan is to ensure that men and women have equal access to and control over the resources needed to implement climate-resilient agricultural production activities in the face of climate-induced rainfall variability and droughts. The specific objectives of the Gender Action Plan are as follows:

(1) To reduce all forms of gender-based discrimination against vulnerable male and female households, especially ethnic minorities, by ensuring that they have access to climate-resilient agricultural production in the face of climate-induced rainfall variability and droughts.

(2) To ensure that men and women, including ethnic minorities, are able to participate equally in decision-making processes related to agriculture and have equal participation, access to and control over resources to implement climate-resilient agricultural production activities.

(3) To build capacity for local partners to identify and address gender inequality issues when implementing climate-resilient agricultural production activities.

170. Propose a focal point at each of the agencies that will be targeted actions to design project activities that are responsive to the special requirements of women and ethnic minorities, but also clear capacity constraints among research agencies to implement such activities and achieve these targets. The focal points should be responsible for ensuring that gender is always kept in mind and with a view to enhance the capacity of those agencies that are mandated to support empowerment.

### Recommendation 1: Undertake systematic institutional capacity building activities

To facilitate equal access by men and women to water for irrigation, localized climate information services, etc., etc.

171. Given the capacity constraints identified among those agencies that are mandated to facilitate women's and ethnic minorities' participation in the project, MARD and UNDP will jointly undertake a series of training sessions for focal points at each agency. The agencies and the tentative topics of training/workshops (to be refined during the inception phase of the project) include:

45

# Annex XIII (c) – GENDER ASSESSMENT AND ACTION PLAN GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL

## Centre for Agriculture Extension

### Topics on training and awareness raising

- Differentiated impact of climate change on men, women, and children
- Review (and development, if needed) of extension materials in the area of agriculture
- Gender sensitive and inclusive facilitation skills
- Collaboration with local champions and VWU members in developing gender-sensitive training modules, diagrams, pictorials for non-literates groups
- Sensitization on climate change awareness and monitoring of the application of climate smart agriculture practices

## Irrigation Sub-departments

### Capacity building activities

- Gender sensitive and inclusive facilitation skills for co-design, costing and O&M of irrigation equipment and distribution systems
- Participatory gender-based analysis of market access requirements and constraints in the design of irrigation schemes and markets. At the same time, institutions like MARD, DARDA, People’s Committee and DOLUSA will be exposed to capacity building/awareness raising activities so that they gain sufficient understanding on the importance of women’s participation
- Development of a detailed strategy for participation of women in irrigation projects
- Participatory gender-based analysis of credit requirements, institutional arrangements and market access
- Development of a plan to monitor status and progress of women's participation

## FU, VWU and Committee for Ethnic Minorities

### Capacity building activities

- Development of a detailed strategy for participation of women in irrigation projects
- Participatory gender-based analysis of credit requirements, institutional arrangements and market access
- Development of a plan to monitor status and progress of women's participation

172. It is important to note that the purpose of these training is not for raising awareness about general needs for women’s empowerment but rather to identify and agree on specific tools, measures and approaches which will be used in the project implementation process to ensure that women and ethnic minorities feel comfortable participating in project activities, voicing their views and concerns, and benefiting from the project outputs. At the same time, institutions like MARD, DARDA, People’s Committee and DOLUSA will be exposed to capacity building/awareness raising activities so that they gain sufficient understanding on the importance of women's participation.

173. While the specific timing of these capacity building activities will be determined later, it is proposed that the first such activity takes place during the inception workshop. The Gender and Sustainable Livelihoods Unit (GSLU) will provide technical support to this workshop with technical assistance from the UNDP Viet Nam Country Office, Bangkok Regional Hub and UNDP HQ. During the inception workshop, the project team will be sensitized on gender issues and updated with the timeline and specific actions aiming at institutional capacity building. After the inception workshop, a series of training sessions will be organized for VWU members and other stakeholders. For example, before community consultations begin for the design of the irrigation and water using technologies, a training session should be held to sensitize VWU members on gender equality, before the commencement of Output 2, staff from the Centre for Agriculture Extension Office will be sensitized.

174. Furthermore, UNDP is currently working with VWU in more than 10 agriculture-related projects supported through the UNDP-GEF Small Grants Programme in nearby provinces (in Bien Dinh, near the Bien Thuan project site). Peer-to-peer learning exchanges targeting VWU members will be organized as part of the implementation of these projects.

# Annex XIII (c) – GENDER ASSESSMENT AND ACTION PLAN

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNGGING PROPOSAL

### Recommendation 2: Assign VWU staff members to provide handholding support to ethnic minority women to enable effective access to water, climate information, credit and markets

175. To ensure effective participation of women, but ethnic minority women in particular, additional and sustained capacity building is required for VWU staff members. This will be provided by identifying local champions, who can play a leadership role among ethnic women; additional awareness sessions with VWU staff to ensure that they are aware of the gender equality principles and that they are clear about what benefits they can gain from participation; identifying additional support needs and gaps in capacity building for VWU staff members. This will be provided through training by UNDP Cambodia where officers from the Provincial Department of Women’s Affairs provided training on how to engage women to join project activities, as well as through a series of capacity building trainings resulting in an increase in participation and consultation in which women had otherwise been dominated by men.

176. The Gender Specialist, with assistance from UNDP, will work with MARD, DADSOs and VWU to agree on the gender action plan. The Specialist will also undertake capacity building activities targeting assigned VWU officers so that they are equipped with key skills, tools and resources for effective implementation of the GAPP. This will include training in gender analysis specific inputs on refining the approach; towards mainstreaming of gender, the Gender specialist will provide guidance to VWU staff members on how to integrate gender into local frameworks of relevant sectors to ensure that strategic opportunities for gender equality in the project phase are identified and addressed. The Specialist will also be responsible for documenting the success stories and gender lessons for replication and policy dialogue in both MARD and DADSOs, as well as with other stakeholders such as UNDP, ADB and GCF. In terms of implementation, aside from capacity building initiatives the Specialist shall fosterment a culture of gender equality by ensuring that all project activities are gender inclusive; objectives, activities, indicators and targets. The specialist shall be actively engaged in developing the Gender Action Plan (GAP) for VWU staff members to ensure that they provide suggestions on specific gender-differentiated implementation strategies and actions in alignment with the Gender Action Plan.

### Recommendation 3: Tailor materials and approaches to meet the unique needs of women and ethnic minorities

177. To improve the effectiveness of project interventions for women and ethnic minorities, the delivery mechanisms may need to be adjusted. For example, the preferred timing of consultations and FGDs with women and ethnic minorities is in the evening when they are more likely to be present before the work plan is finalized. To this end, the VWU support structure described above may be adjusted accordingly. In addition, the design of materials for use by women and ethnic minorities will need to be adapted; especially for ethnic minorities and non-literate groups. DADSOs will work with VWU staff members in developing these materials. The same applies to FGD designs and, necessary training will be offered to relevant staff who will be delivering the FGD sessions. In addition, it is recommended that a gender liaison person between lead farmers and trainers are two of the additional measures that are currently considered.

178. Opportunities and mechanisms to promote women’s participation in social groups and climate resilience activities need to be identified and implemented. These include: making meetings more convenient; providing group members from the Women’s Union; providing materials in local languages and in accessible formats; structuring training and other groups in ways that make women feel comfortable, for example by having separate sessions or smaller group sizes at levels to assist women to participate. These mechanisms are found in the recommendations in the GAP.

47

# Annex XIII (c) – GENDER ASSESSMENT AND ACTION PLAN

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL

### Recommendation 4: Promote equality in decision making

179. At community level, the project will establish water users' groups and pond management groups to ensure that women have equal access to resources and promotes equality between men and women in making decisions in agricultural production and investment. Within these groups, the project will support the establishment of women's sub-groups or committees where women can share their experiences and learn from each other. Women's leadership and agency will be considered to create and strengthen an enabling environment for women's empowerment at community level.

180. At household level, the project will support men and women to implement water efficient irrigation practices in their households through appropriate markets. These will be considered as a means to promote open discussion between men and women regarding household-level water use.

181. In addition, the project will support both men and women to access loan services by strengthening available lending mechanisms to ensure that women farmers can receive this service on equal terms with men. The project will also support the establishment of women in discussion and negotiation with men in making decisions on loans and investment within the household. Women's Union or Farmers Unions at provincial and district level, as well as those provided and managed by the Women's Union and Farmers Unions can be also considered.

182. These specific measures and approaches presented above will be discussed with relevant stakeholders during the inception phase of the project, and particularly at the inception workshop. The Gender Action Plan (GAP) will be developed in close collaboration with these agencies to operationalize these actions. Moreover, simple check-lists will be produced to support agencies to help them provide gender equality in their work. These checklists will also ensure that gender equality cuts across all cutting of the design and implementation of the project, and thus gender and ethnic minorities are not left behind. The specific measures and approaches presented above do not only generalize awareness raising about gender equality and ethnic minority issues, but also address the specific responsibilities in the project. A focal point on gender and ethnic minorities from each of these agencies will be identified to ensure that they are fully involved in all aspects of the project. State agencies who are responsible for gender equality issues, as well as development actors who have experience with gender equality and ethnic minority issues (e.g., Women's Union, Indian Ethnic Minority at provincial and district level; Women's Union at provincial and district level; Department of Labor, Involve and Social Affairs, and NGOs who are working in the project provinces.

## Proposed gender action plan (GAP) and budget

The purpose of this Gender Action Plan is to operationalize the constraints and opportunities for women and men that were identified during the above gender analysis, towards fully integrating them into the design and implementation phases of the proposed Green Climate Fund funded climate change mitigation project. In the context of the project, within the beneficiary population, the concentration of women 48

# Annex XIII (c) – GENDER ASSESSMENT AND ACTION PLAN

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL

Headed households at 55% is prominent. The project as a whole, and the sub-activities therein are geared to address nuances encompassing both males and females. Consequently, the project sub-activities are designed in such a way that they are gender-sensitive and inclusive of both male and female activities; however, that are targeted primarily to women in keeping with the assessment's recommendations outlined in the GAP, for example, in promoting interventions in local languages (Activity 1.3), or scheduling of meetings (Activity 2.1) to enhance women’s participation. Specific indicators are also proposed to measure and track progress on these interventions at the activity level, which will be reviewed during the project implementation phase as well as at the end of implementation. The M&E plan proposes concrete actions to ensure that the degree of gender-responsiveness is maintained (including collection of sex and age disaggregated data) continues to be measured throughout implementation.

It should also be noted that the project take into consideration gender and social inclusion measures outlined above and tailored specifically for women and households headed by women. A specific budget has been allocated to ensure that women or households headed by women and ethnic minorities among total beneficiaries will receive support from the project as well as strengthen their participation in the project activities. The budget is estimated at 10% of the total cost of the project, which is included in the total cost of the project and is in alignment with the activities undertaken within the project based on recommendation and considerations from the GAP.
